Added authoring/runtime geometry is 672 vertices and 640 polygons with one owned material.
- The locked tone gate against r496 passes: forehead 0.000000%, left cheek -0.000685%, right cheek -0.000164% and whole face -0.000379%. Eleven matched views expose no pose break, floating lower-lash band or new silhouette failure.
- r499 addresses the remaining uniform bright tear line. The source material uses alpha 0.24, transmission 0.28 and coat 0.55 under `DITHERED` surface rendering. The selected study keeps every physical socket value and all geometry unchanged, changing only the surface method to sorted `BLENDED`; darker/thinner alternatives are rejected because they add a pink lower-lid shadow or erase the wet response.
- r499 repeats the r498 collision, static-parity, dynamic-hair, tone and 11-view gates and passes all of them. Strict scores are eyes 68/100, face 56/100, mouth 53/100, hair 38/100, body/clothing 49/100 and overall 52/100. The hero gate remains failed: the surviving upper lashes still lack natural macro-clumps, orbital fat and lid thickness are simplified, and hair mass, skin secondary breakup and garments remain well below hero quality.
- r499 becomes the retained offline technical mainline. Website v83 remains unchanged; website performance is not claimed until a later runtime candidate is exported and benchmarked. The next eye pass must rebuild upper lashes as irregular macro-clumps without the rejected comb pattern, then development returns to the larger hair-mass blocker.

### v249r500–r504 — upper-lash and hair-structure studies (rejected)

- r500 rebuilds 16/19/22 upper-lash macro-clumps from collision-fitted eyelid boundary samples. Every bound clears the eye layers at frames 1/20, but the new roots and fibers disappear into the lid surface and leave skin-colored folds instead of readable lashes. The existing 38-fiber upper layers remain retained.
- r501 preserves all accepted roots and groups only the fiber tips. Paired, natural and strong grouping bounds pass the same collision checks, but each produces black loops, stitches or hooks along the lid margin. Tip convergence is rejected as the wrong construction method.
- r499 runtime inspection records 1,392 dynamic cards: 420 front, 420 crown, 480 rear and 72 hairline cards. The layers have complete armature groups and enough longitudinal rows for deformation; the visible blocker is shared planar flow, uniform opacity and insufficient macro-lock separation.
- r502 tests 2/5/8 mm coherent card displacement. The 2 mm bound passes four-frame body and garment clearance but is visually negligible. The 5 and 8 mm bounds modestly widen the silhouette yet introduce front/crown penetration, so no volume-only variant is promoted.
- r503 adds sampled broad foundation cards below the accepted groom. All three bounds pass frames 1/20/33/61, but they only increase opacity and perceived darkness. They do not remove the rectangular rear veil or create readable locks, matching the user's observation that the character appears darker without becoming more realistic.
- r504 moves tapered, curved macro cards to the hair surface. The conservative rear-only bound passes collision but remains too subtle; balanced and strong bounds show slightly more breakup yet penetrate during motion and retain the central flat curtain. The complete sweep is rejected.
- r499 remains the offline mainline and website v83 remains unchanged. The next hair foundation must replace the rear curtain topology with authored guide families and staggered silhouettes, rather than layering or offsetting derivatives of the same planar cards.

### v249r505–r506 — direct layered rear-lock replacement

- r505 stops layering geometry over the existing rear curtain. It hides the 480-card rear layer only inside each study variant and rebuilds 160/220/280-card replacements from spatially distributed owned roots, grouped centerline curves, denser owned atlas slots and deliberately staggered lengths. Every bound passes body and garment collision at frames 1/20/33/61.
- The 160-card bound is too sparse and the 220-card bound leaves excessive side gaps. The selected 280-card bound removes the long rectangular center veil while retaining enough coverage and gives the clearest shorter, rounded and uneven bob silhouette.
- r506 records the replacement as an explicit `hair.runtime_layered_locks.rear.dynamic.alpha` layer. The old rear object remains in the authoring file, is hidden in viewport/render, and points to its replacement. All pre-existing mesh hashes match r499 exactly. Visible rear cost falls by 200 cards, 9,600 authoring vertices and 4,600 quads.
- The formal dynamic audit passes against the body, bandeau, bandeau binding and shorts at frames 1/20/33/61 with zero penetrating vertices. Rear P95 motion is 2.666 mm, peak motion is 4.631 mm, maximum root drift is 0.000017 mm and frame-61 settle error is 0.000031 mm.
- The fixed tone contract passes. Central skin changes by +0.0288%, left/right cheeks by +0.0001%/+0.0005% and forehead by +0.0014%; the rear hair context becomes 15.125% brighter than r499, so the replacement does not continue the darkening trend.
- A same-material r499/r506 runtime export and shared-renderer browser test alternates five 300-frame runs at 900×900. r506 keeps 14 draw calls, reduces triangles from 141,968 to 123,568 and download size from 8.267 MB to 7.255 MB, matches the 8.6 ms median parse time and reduces median render time from 0.1073 to 0.1047 ms. Node.js structural/load checks also pass.
- Eleven matched views show no new body, clothing, face or pose artifact. Under the current stricter rubric, hair rises from 38 to 45/100 and overall character from 52 to 53/100. Crown symmetry, planar front/side sheets, weak large/medium/fine lock hierarchy and uniform flyaways still fail the hero gate.
- r506 becomes the retained offline technical mainline. Website v83 remains unchanged and website promotion is not approved. The next pass rebuilds crown and side flow as independently authored overlapping lock families while preserving the r506 collision, tone and browser-performance contracts.

### v249r507–r510 — rejected crown controls and lighter neutral skin

- r507 directly replaces the 420-card crown with 240/300/340-card shortened, widened and curved groups. The matched front, three-quarter, profile, top and frame-33 views show little useful change, while every candidate introduces dynamic body penetration with 17.0–19.9 mm worst signed depth. All r507 variants are rejected.
- r508 isolates center-part root width from the other variables. An identity rebuild plus 1.15× and 1.30× center-root bounds preserve the complete r506 guide centerlines, lengths, UVs, weights, modifiers and transforms. All three pass body and garment collision at frames 1/20/33/61, but the visible difference is negligible. This proves the broad straight part and planar sides cannot be repaired by root-width tuning alone.
- r509 holds geometry, lights, exposure, hair and clothing fixed while testing four melanin-ramp pairs. The selected `b-lighter-neutral` response removes the brown/dim cast while preserving facial plane separation. The next two bounds increasingly flatten or wash out the face and are rejected.
- r510 records that selection as a material-only offline technical candidate. All mesh hashes, armatures, shader node/link counts, textures, material slots, modifiers, triangles and draw calls remain invariant against r506. The four-frame runtime-hair/body/clothing collision audit passes with the exact r506 motion envelope.
- Under Standard/None with exposure 0 and the fixed review lights, scene-linear luminance increases by 26.717% on the forehead, 24.792% on the left cheek, 25.525% on the right cheek and 24.846% on the chin. Eleven views expose no new artifact. Strict scores are skin 57/100, face geometry 56/100, hair 45/100 and overall 54/100.
- r510 becomes the retained offline technical mainline. The hero gate remains failed and website v83 remains unchanged. The next hair pass must author asymmetric crown guides and grouped side locks; the next skin/face pass needs regional maps and stronger eyelid, orbital and perioral secondary anatomy.

### v249r511–r517 — visible-layer attribution and layered front locks

- r511 tests coherent asymmetric crown direction, lift and part crossing. Only the 4 mm bound passes collision and its full-groom change is negligible; 7–10 mm bounds still look planar and fail dynamic clearance. r512 reduces and widens crown cards; all bounds pass collision but remain visually hidden.
- r513 isolates the seven visible runtime hair layers under identical cameras. It proves `AevryndFemaleRuntimeCards_front_R467` creates nearly the entire visible front/side curtain. Crown contributes only a narrow top strip beneath it, while the r506 rear layer supplies back and lower-rear coverage. This changes the target from crown tuning to front-layer replacement.
- r514 replaces the 420-card front layer with 260/320/380 broader, curved and length-staggered locks. The new grouping is visible, but all initial bounds have shallow 1.9–3.2 mm body penetration. r515 preserves exact source vertices while reducing density; it passes collision but creates stepped temple/ear holes and is rejected.
- r516 applies the deepest animated body-contact correction to complete card tails in head space over frames 1/20/33/61. The 260 and 320 bounds converge to zero penetration; 380 cards retain collision after five passes. The 320-card middle bound is selected because it retains coverage without the 260-card isolated long strips.
- r517 records that selection as `hair.runtime_layered_locks.front.dynamic.alpha`. The old front object remains hidden and points to its replacement. Every pre-existing mesh hash stays exact; visible front cost falls by 100 cards, 4,800 authoring vertices and 2,300 quads.
- The full runtime audit passes against body, bandeau, binding and shorts across all four frames. Maximum layer P95 motion remains 2.666 mm, peak maximum remains 4.631 mm, and root/settle drift remains negligible. Fixed skin regions change by only -0.029% to -0.349%; left/right hair context becomes 3.592%/17.348% brighter.
- The r517 runtime GLB is 6,748,776 bytes versus r506 at 7,254,764 bytes. Node.js reports 9,200 fewer vertices and 4,600 fewer triangles with seven draw calls unchanged. An actual Chrome WebGL test alternates five 300-frame batches at 900×900: rendered triangles fall from 123,568 to 114,368, draw calls remain 14, median parse falls from 9.6 to 7.5 ms and median render falls from 0.1017 to 0.0987 ms.
- Eleven matched views show more uneven side grouping and lower density without a new pose artifact. Strict hair rises from 45 to 47/100 and overall character to 55/100. The remaining front mass is still too straight, the part is still symmetric, macro/medium/fine hierarchy is weak and flyaways are insufficient, so the hero gate remains failed and website v83 is unchanged.
### v249r518–r520 — facial contrast rejection and natural eye-ratio mainline

- r518 holds geometry, lights, exposure, eye materials and the r510 skin fixed while sweeping brow-fiber bevel from 0.038mm to 0.066mm and upper/lower vermilion target color. The differences are too small to repair the face, while stronger bounds begin to read as makeup. The study is retained as a visual reject rather than promoted.
- r519 measures the inherited open-eye boundary at about 22.06mm wide by 12.98mm high, an aspect ratio of 1.70 that produces a round stare. A continuous-body sweep moves 2,235 eye-neighborhood vertices together across all six shape keys. Ratios 2.04, 2.23 and 2.44 are compared under matched views; 2.23 is selected because 2.04 retains too much roundness and 2.44 reads as a squint.
- r520 carries the selected lid displacement into both 760-vertex upper-lash layers and 177 points of the conforming eye shell. It removes only cornea/sclera faces hidden inside the reshaped lids. Whole radial iris-face deletion is rejected because it creates white wedges; a 12.5mm iris recess is rejected because the iris disappears. The retained irises keep all 96 faces per side and use a collision-safe local-axis fit of 0.85/0.65/0.60.
- The static/face audit passes with only the expected cornea, sclera, upper-lash and conforming-shell mesh changes plus the body shape-key coordinates. Every eye and mouth layer has zero body overlap at frames 1/20/33/61. Runtime hair retains zero body/bandeau/binding/shorts penetration, maximum layer P95 motion 2.666mm, maximum motion 4.631mm, negligible root drift and full settle.
- The tone contract passes against r517: forehead +0.000499%, left cheek +0.000772%, right cheek +0.001573% and chin +0.000069%. Structural performance also passes with 148 fewer visible vertices, 687 fewer polygons, 1,374 fewer triangles, unchanged material count and unchanged draw-call upper bound.
- Strict scores are hair 47/100, eyes 69/100, face 57/100, skin 57/100, mouth 53/100, body/clothing 49/100 and overall character 56/100. r520 becomes the offline technical mainline, but the hero gate still fails on orbital fat, lid thickness, tear-duct/wetness response, mouth commissures, skin microdetail and hair macro hierarchy. Website v83 remains unchanged.

### v249r521–r522 — continuous commissures and restrained lip volume

- r521 keeps r520 materials, lights, eyes, hair and clothing fixed while sweeping a continuous-body perioral field. It recesses and slightly narrows both commissures, separates paired upper-lip peaks from the central notch, and adds a restrained lower-lip tubercle. The first 0.55–1.05 bounds are too subtle at face distance; 1.25–2.25 bounds establish the useful visual range, with 1.75 selected for formal testing and 2.25 rejected as pinched.
- The first r522 build at strength 1.75 moves 1,786 vertices with 0.814mm maximum displacement and passes geometry, face-layer, body-self-intersection and runtime-hair collision. It fails the fixed chin luminance gate at +1.060%, so it is rejected rather than weakening the gate.
- The final r522 reduces strength to 1.60, moving 1,782 mouth-neighborhood vertices with 0.744mm maximum displacement across all six shape keys. Mesh topology, materials, runtime dependencies, visible vertices, triangles and draw-call upper bound remain unchanged.
- Frames 1/20/33/61 show no body-self-intersection regression and zero eye, mouth-cavity, tongue or upper-teeth overlap. Runtime hair retains zero body/bandeau/binding/shorts penetration, 2.666mm maximum layer P95 motion, 4.631mm peak motion, stable roots and full settle.
- The fixed tone contract passes against r520: forehead -0.000004%, left cheek -0.000333%, right cheek +0.000534% and chin +0.972793%. Matched static and frame-20 mouth views show a clearer soft commissure dimple and slightly fuller central lower lip without a smile or new pose artifact.
- Strict scores are mouth 55/100, face 58/100 and overall character 57/100; eyes remain 69, skin 57, hair 47 and body/clothing 49. r522 becomes the offline technical mainline. Broad philtrum/upper-lip transition, weak fine lip creases and moisture breakup, orbital volume, skin microdetail and hair hierarchy still fail the hero gate. Website v83 remains unchanged.

### v249r523–r524 — rejected orbital-volume and eye-envelope study

- r523 preserves the r520 eye aperture and its immediate 2.2 mm attachment ring while testing upper/lower lid rolls, upper crease, tear-trough and malar transitions on 2,060 continuous-body vertices across all six shape keys. Strengths 1.8, 2.3 and 2.8 make the relief visible, but the stronger bound begins to read as a tired lower-eye ring.
- The first r524 signed-relief build intersects both cornea and sclera at frames 1/20/33/61. Removing all inward crease and tear-trough motion does not solve it; the outward-only lid rolls increase overlap because the retained r520 optical envelope is already tightly tangent to the body opening.
- A six-bound collision sweep tests strengths 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.40, 0.80 and 1.20. Even 0.05, with only about 0.017 mm maximum movement, creates two eye-layer overlap pairs at every frame. Moving the complete optical stack along either world-Y direction increases contact and eventually introduces iris, tear-meniscus or lash overlap.
- No nonzero strength satisfies the zero-overlap requirement, so r523/r524 is rejected and r522 remains the offline mainline. The next orbital attempt must rebuild local socket clearance/topology and then refit every optical layer; displacement over the current envelope is closed as an invalid route. Website v83 remains unchanged.

### v249r525–r526 — collision-safe orbital relief and brow refit

- r525 measures the 52-point eye boundaries against the evaluated optical surfaces. Minimum clearance is only 0.021 mm left and 0.013 mm right, with about 0.40 mm median clearance, explaining why every direct r524 displacement collides.
- The selected r523 signed relief is rebuilt, then only cornea/sclera faces hidden behind the displaced lids across frames 1/20/33/61 are removed: 51/34 faces left and 43/37 right. All iris faces remain. The full face/self-intersection audit passes with zero eye or mouth-layer overlap and no body regression.
- r525 initially pushes the existing brow fibers into the new upper orbital surface. r526 projects 135 left and 102 right control points to 0.13 mm target clearance, moving them by at most 0.252/0.230 mm. All four frames then pass with zero brow/body overlap, 0.128/0.129 mm minimum root clearance and only 0.000023 mm maximum head-space drift.
- Runtime hair retains zero body/bandeau/binding/shorts penetration with the same 2.666 mm maximum layer P95 and 4.631 mm peak motion. Structural cost falls by 163 visible vertices, 165 polygons and 330 triangles; material and draw-call bounds are unchanged.
- The fixed tone contract passes versus r522: forehead +0.0945%, left cheek +0.1056%, right cheek +0.0721% and chin +0.00005%, so this pass does not darken the face. Eleven views and close A/B views show modestly clearer lid/orbital volume without a new optical hole.
- A full-character Chrome WebGL benchmark alternates five 300-frame batches at 900×900. r526 falls from 16,222,608 to 16,214,800 bytes, 391,126 to 390,278 triangles, keeps 70 draw calls, lowers median render time from 0.4150 to 0.4097 ms and median parse time from 27.4 to 23.1 ms. All gates pass.
- Strict scores become eyes 70/100, face 59/100 and overall 58/100; hair remains 47, skin 57, mouth 55 and body/clothing 49. r526 becomes the offline technical mainline, but synthetic eyelid margins/canthi, weak tear response, flat skin microdetail and planar hair remain hero-gate failures. Website v83 remains unchanged.

### v249r527–r531 — front-hair hierarchy and source-groom studies (rejected)

- r527 measures 320 visible front cards with 24 longitudinal samples each. Their roots and lengths are highly regular and nearly mirrored around the center part. Coherent 14-degree grouping, five-level width hierarchy, length staggering and depth/lateral offsets remain a straight planar curtain in matched front, oblique, profile and top views.
- r528 keeps one material and draw-call family while reducing fill alpha by hierarchy. Lower opacity only exposes ears and scalp; it neither creates large locks nor improves the silhouette. The sparse bound looks bald and is rejected.
- r529 averages cards into 22–53 macro guides and renders 66–106 wide primary ribbons above a reduced-opacity fill. Oblique, profile and top views expose triangular flat patches. Treating macro guides as visible geometry is closed as an invalid route.
- r530 instead builds 5,120 fine procedural fibres from distributed Aevrynd-owned card roots. Fibres converge only through the midshaft and reopen at staggered tips. The paper-card edge is reduced, but inherited straight centerlines still produce an evenly combed curtain and the strand shader makes the hair mass read darker.
- Fixed front-view crops confirm that r526 skin itself was not darkened: r526-to-r530 differences are only -0.004% on the forehead, about -0.086%/-0.087% on the cheeks and -0.010% on the chin. The perceived darkening comes from the adjacent hair value, specular response and occluding mass.
- r531 adds coherent nonparallel bows, depth offsets and shared group-length hierarchy to the fine fibres. It changes the ends but retains a helmet-like top and parallel side mass; stronger bounds increase irregular tips without producing believable large/medium/fine locks. r527–r531 are visual rejects, r526 remains the offline technical mainline, and website v83 remains unchanged.
- The next groom must start from a small manually placed set of scalp-to-tip silhouette guides with explicit part, temple, side and rear regions. Child roots can be generated from the scalp, but inherited r517 card centerlines cannot define the macro shape. The selected guide groom must then be baked into narrow overlapping cards and pass four-frame body/clothing collision plus browser cost gates.

### v249r532–r533 — manual silhouette guides and root-fill study (rejected)

- r532 retains the 320 collision-safe root regions but replaces every inherited long centerline with 21 explicit scalp-to-tip cubic guides. The 0.45 bound produces a shorter and less rectangular side silhouette; 0.65 and 0.82 expose large part/temple holes and collapse fibres into coarse separated regions. None reaches a believable groom.
- r533 pairs the least aggressive r532 strand bound with only the first 4/7/10 longitudinal segments of the r517 cards. Four and seven rows read as overlapping scales in top and oblique views. Ten rows close more gaps but visibly restore the old planar curtain and symmetric part.
- Both studies are visual rejects. They prove that even truncated old cards cannot serve as the visible root foundation. The next implementation must sample roots directly on the owned scalp, author the hairline and density transition separately, then generate primary locks and fill fibres from those roots before any runtime-card bake. r526/r517 remain the retained technical baselines and website v83 is unchanged.

### v249r534–r539 — owned-scalp groom reconstruction (research only)

- r534 cuts the dependency on r517 long-card centerlines. It raycasts 122 primary roots and every child root onto the evaluated owned body at 2 mm clearance, then generates 2,928/4,880/6,832 fibres. The root field is valid, but fibres leave the scalp too early and collapse into wet-looking cables and concentric top bands; all density bounds fail visually.
- r535 keeps the first eight controls on 30–40 mm of the scalp surface and retains 55% of each lock cross-section after release. This removes the worst cables and establishes a continuous side mass, but regular root rows remain visible as horizontal comb marks.
- r536 jitters root rows and varies surface-follow direction and release distance. It weakens the repeated top pattern without changing total density, yet 122 guides still form coarse bundles and do not establish convincing large/medium/fine hierarchy.
- r537 holds total fibres near 5–6k while testing 176, 282 and 421 independently flowing scalp guides. The 421-guide/5,894-fibre bound gives the smoothest crown and best coverage of this series, but its hairline is painted and hard, side lengths are blunt, and the back remains a rounded cap. Its strict hair score is 46/100, below retained r517 at 47/100, so it is research input rather than a candidate.
- r538 moves long roots 12/20/28 mm above the anatomical hairline and adds 130 separate transition fibres. The long-hair edge softens, but the transition roots form a dotted chain across the forehead. All three bounds are rejected.
- r539 locks r537 geometry, cameras, exposure and lights while sweeping Principled Hair melanin 0.44/0.52/0.60. The lighter bounds reduce the adjacent dark mass but cannot hide geometry defects; 0.52 is retained only as the next groom's neutral-brown material target. No r534–r539 version is promoted, no collision/performance claim is made for these visual studies, r526/r517 remain technical baselines, and website v83 is unchanged.

### v249r540–r543 — tail hierarchy, root taper and first owned card bake

- r540 replaces independent random tail lengths with shared 3×3 macro and 2×2 medium endpoint height, fan and depth. Strength 1.35 gives the clearest grouped front/side silhouette; weaker bounds average back toward a straight cut. The hard root edge remains.
- r541 combines r540 strength 1.35 with r539 melanin 0.52 and grows fibre radius from 15/35/55% to full width over the first 16% of each strand. The 15% bound gives the softest hairline without the dotted r538 chain. Strict source-groom hair quality reaches 48/100, one point above r517, but it is still an offline source study with no secondary motion, card bake, dynamic collision or browser-performance proof.
- r542 bakes the same 421 guides to 842 two-layer cards at 1.8/2.5/3.2 mm using the inherited atlas. All variants are too transparent and expose the ears, scalp and neck. Wider cards increase empty alpha area rather than coverage, so the inherited atlas is incompatible with the new guide topology.
- r543 generates a deterministic 1024×1024 four-slot Aevrynd-owned colour/alpha atlas with no external pixels and repeats the bake at 3.2/4.0/5.0 mm. The texture loads correctly, but isolated one-pixel fibres produce sparse grey/white specular layers and insufficient opacity. Every card bound is rejected.
- The next atlas must be rendered from dense clump geometry into coverage alpha, tangent-direction normal and roughness maps, with root/tip masks matched to card width. Only after the r541 silhouette survives that bake should the card mesh receive armature weights, secondary motion, four-frame collision and browser gates. r526/r517 remain the technical mainlines and website v83 remains unchanged.

### v249r544–r547 — dense clump maps and cross-card orientation (rejected bake)

- r544 deterministically generates a second Aevrynd-owned 1024×1024 four-slot atlas set: colour/coverage alpha, tangent-direction normal and roughness. It uses no external pixels and raises nontrivial alpha coverage to about 75.39% across the atlas.
- r545 tests 421 single-layer 5 mm cards and 842 two-layer 3.5/4.5 mm cards. Coverage is much stronger than r543, proving the new alpha is read correctly, but the colour/normal response washes the cards toward pink-grey and top views expose separated fishbone strokes.
- r546 holds the selected two-layer 3.5 mm geometry and alpha fixed, reduces normal strength to 0.08, specular to 0.15, removes coat and sweeps colour multipliers 0.18/0.28/0.40. The darkest bound restores a brown value but does not remove the structural fishbone pattern or weak side coverage, so the failure is not a tone-only issue.
- r547 derives a local orthogonal width basis from every guide tangent and compares parallel two-layer cards, 90-degree crossed two-layer cards and 60-degree crossed three-layer cards. Crossing improves oblique/rear coverage as expected, but 1,263 cards still expose discrete atlas strokes and remain far below the r541 source groom.
- All r545–r547 bakes are rejected. Cross-card orientation is retained as a valid runtime requirement; the r544 atlas shape is rejected. The next bake needs a continuous irregular clump core with internal strand breakup and separated tips, rather than hundreds of isolated full-length lines. Website v83 and the r526/r517 technical mainlines remain unchanged.

### v249r548–r550 — continuous clump atlas and hybrid crown tests (rejected)

- r548 replaces isolated full-length strokes with four deterministic Aevrynd-owned filled clump cores, short internal separations, boundary fibres and tapered split tips. Alpha coverage above 8 is 58.66%; no external pixels are used.
- r549 holds the 421-guide root field and compares 842 crossed cards at 3.5/5.0mm with 1,263 three-way cards at 4.5mm. Filled clumps remove the pale sparse result, but each complete guide still reads as a separate layered strip and the top view exposes every root direction.
- r550 retains the collision-safe r526 crown and hairline while replacing only front/rear long-hair layers. Widths from 3.5 to 6.5mm do not hide the repeated card origins; the retained layers are an underlay rather than a sufficient root occluder.
- Reject r549/r550 below r517's 47/100 hair gate. Next build must cluster guides into a smaller number of semantic macro locks, use overlapping fill cards only between those locks, and prevent any full-length one-guide/one-card mapping. Website v83 remains unchanged.

### v249r551–r557 — semantic-card rejection and complete five-view source groom

- r551 clusters 421 guides into 64/96/128 macro locks plus 120/160/200 shortened fill cards. Card counts fall to 248/352/456, but averaged wide guides fold into flat broken strips and expose large root gaps. The automatic guide-to-wide-card route is rejected.
- r552 returns to native hair curves and applies local three-child clumping, separated tips and cumulative noise in the same order learned from Blender's official reference. Strength 0.78 improves tail grouping without breaking the crown, but the original four-view review misses a severe posterior-centre gap.
- r553 adds the mandatory back camera and tests a regional lob cut. The back view exposes a large rectangular bare occipital region, invalidating the provisional score and proving that all future groom reviews require front, three-quarter, profile, back and top views.
- r554 extends posterior root sampling from y=0.076m to 0.100/0.118m. Root count reaches 429, but coverage remains open because the new guides still split toward the left/right shell targets.
- r555 redirects rear-family endpoints toward their own root x coordinate. The gap narrows from a rectangle to a vertical strip, proving the direction diagnosis, but central density is still insufficient.
- r556 adds a separately named occipital fill family with surface-following roots and vertically falling tails. The 6x11 bound reaches 495 guides/6,930 fibres and closes the posterior gap across all five views without introducing a new front, side or top hole.
- r557 locks r556 geometry and fixed review lighting while sweeping only Principled Hair melanin. The 0.44 bound preserves the most side/rear strand detail and reduces perceived face darkening; 0.48/0.52 progressively crush the hair value. Retain r556 geometry plus 0.44 only as a source-groom research baseline at about 50/100. It has no dynamic collision, motion, runtime-card or browser-performance proof, so r517/r526 and website v83 remain unchanged.

### v249r558–r559 — covered-groom regional-shape studies

- r558 keeps the r556 495-guide/6,930-fibre coverage and r557 melanin 0.44 fixed while varying regional crown lift, face framing and temple tuck. Strength 0.65 gives only a small improvement; 1.0 concentrates the crown and shortens the side mass. Neither bound resolves the rounded helmet silhouette.
- r559 holds the 0.65 regional field and tests guide-wave strengths 1.0, 1.8 and 2.6. The middle bound is visible only at close range, while 2.6 synchronizes neighbouring guides into an artificial repeated wave. No variant is promoted or baked into runtime cards.
- r556+r557 remains the owned source-groom research baseline at about 50/100. It still has no dynamics, card bake, collision or browser proof; r517 remains the collision-tested runtime hair baseline at 47/100.

### v249r560–r569 — lower-face proportion and canthus-topology investigation

- r560 tests jaw contraction, malar projection and chin-height fields across all six body shape keys. The balanced 3.0/1.4/3.0 mm control is visually restrained and preferable to the pointed strong bound, but it is treated as research until optical clearance is proven.
- r561 moves 326 eyelid-neighbourhood vertices toward an almond aperture. Even the 25% bound moves points by up to 1.124 mm without a meaningful visual gain, so the complete sweep is rejected.
- r562-r565 progressively hide lashes, tear layers, caruncles, catchlights and finally every `Eye_*` object. The outer triangular canthus remains visible with an empty socket, proving it comes from the continuous body's 52-vertex open eye boundary and adjacent triangles rather than an optical overlay.
- r566 applies bounded X/Z Laplacian fairing around the outer canthi. Three iterations visibly reduce the spike; five iterations overflatten the corner. When the selected three-iteration result is integrated in r567, both cornea and sclera overlap the body in every tested frame, so the apparently cleaner image is rejected as a collision failure.
- r568 removes canthus fairing but leaves the lower-face field active up to 1.625 m. Its small high-zone falloff still intersects both corneas and sclerae at frames 1/20/33/61, proving that a visually negligible tail can violate the zero-overlap gate.
- r569 hard-clamps the lower-face field below 1.588 m. It moves 6,891 vertices across all six shape keys, with 1.796 mm maximum displacement and no topology, material or runtime dependency change. Every eye, tear, lash and mouth layer has zero body overlap at frames 1/20/33/61, body self-intersection counts remain at the r526 baseline, and all runtime hair/body/clothing penetration counts are zero.
- Under identical camera, lights and Standard/None exposure, r569 changes fixed scene-linear skin luminance by +0.038% forehead, +0.055% left cheek, +0.083% right cheek and +0.481% chin. It does not darken the skin. Eleven views show a modestly cleaner jaw/malar/lower-third balance without a new pose or silhouette break.
- r569 becomes the retained offline face/character technical candidate. Strict face score is 60/100 and overall remains 58/100 because the gain is small; eyes remain 70, skin 57, mouth 55, hair 47 and body/clothing 49. Synthetic eyelid margins, triangular canthus topology, flat skin breakup and planar hair still fail the hero gate. Website v83 remains unchanged.

### v249r570–r574 — sparse-guide interpolation and regional hair architecture

- Local inspection of Blender 5.2's bundled procedural-hair node assets identifies the modern separation used by `Generate Hair Curves`, `Interpolate Hair Curves`, `Clump Hair Curves`, `Hair Curves Noise` and `Set Hair Curve Profile`: sparse guides define flow, coverage is interpolated, guide indices control clumps, noise is a later operation and radius has its own profile. No Blender node group or runtime dependency is copied into Aevrynd.
- r570 keeps all 495 roots and 6,930 fibres but moves coarse spatial families together. The first bound breaks symmetry, while stronger bounds create top tufts, loops and side/rear holes. Whole-family displacement is rejected.
- r571 selects 84 sparse guides and interpolates all 495 surface-root trajectories from three nearby compatible guides, producing 5,940 child curves. Front grouping improves slightly, but global clumping opens severe rear holes from 0.22 upward. The interpolation architecture is useful; global application is rejected.
- r572 separates the hair system by function: the 6x11 occipital family remains a complete coverage layer while guide-index clumping is limited to front/side roots. A 0.22 front clump with 0.55 temple tuck keeps rear/top coverage and avoids the r571 holes, but the visible improvement is still too small for promotion.
- r573 widens the light-side ear tuck to the entire anterior half. It exposes one ear, but top and front views reveal a horizontal wing before the hair turns behind the head. All r573 bounds are rejected.
- r574 adds 3.5/6.0/9.0 mm loose waves after interpolation, with phase and frequency varying per sparse guide and small within-clump offsets. The waves avoid synchronized rows and keep roots fixed, but dense coverage averages the lower bounds away; 9 mm only frays the outline. No r570-r574 variant exceeds the retained source-groom score of about 50/100.
- The next source groom must contain a sparse, explicitly visible outer-lock layer over separate medium fill and occipital coverage layers. Outer locks need authored silhouettes and cross-sections before child interpolation; more displacement or noise on the dense coverage layer is closed as an invalid route.

### v249r575 — explicit outer-lock extraction (rejected)

- r575 retains r572 as a dedicated 5,940-curve coverage underlayer and creates a separately named outer-lock object and material. Sparse, balanced and dense bounds use 16/21/31 sampled outer guides, 288/378/558 child curves, 4/6/8 mm radial lift, a 1.32 radius multiplier and melanin 0.36.
- The sparse and balanced layers remain visually submerged. The dense layer finally reads separately, but top and front views show raised loops and arcs because the outer guides inherit coverage-layer trajectories. No bound creates authored lock silhouettes or a believable cross-section, so the whole sweep is rejected without collision or runtime promotion.
- The next outer layer must be sculpted from independent control curves and explicit ribbon/ellipse cross-sections. Sampling, lifting or recolouring coverage guides is closed as an invalid macro-lock construction method.

### v249r576–r577 — independent outer trajectories and alpha-card cross-sections (rejected)

- r576 authors 12/16/20 outer trajectories independently from the 5,940-curve r572 coverage layer. The controls span front, side, crown and rear regions, but expanding each path into 28 round fibres produces thick cables, raised crown arcs and loop silhouettes. Independence alone is insufficient when the visible cross-section is circular and overbuilt.
- r577 keeps the independent trajectories but reduces their release arches and replaces each round bundle with one tapered two-sided alpha card. The owned four-slot RGBA atlas contains 28–37 warm-brown fine strands per slot with transparent gaps and staggered tips. The coverage layer also changes from melanin 0.44 to 0.39 with greater redness so the test does not hide form in near-black values.
- Fixed front-crop luminance confirms that the face itself is stable at 141.30–141.33. Adding the r572 coverage architecture lowers the larger head crop from 103.06 to 101.72, explaining the perceived darkening; r577 cards recover only 0.09–0.10 points. Global exposure is therefore not the correction.
- r577 removes r576's rope silhouette, but the top and rear views expose bright parallel card strips and root slots. All bounds are rejected. The next pass needs narrower cards aligned to scalp tangents, softer root alpha, staggered depth and explicit overlap order while keeping the fixed face-lighting contract.

### v249r578–r584 — fixed hair-value contract and multiframe collision envelope

- r578 narrows the outer layer to 5.0/4.0/3.4 mm cards, aligns card roots to the scalp tangent, fades the atlas to zero at the root and staggers 32/40-card companion layers by 2.2/1.8 mm. The r577 bright top strips disappear, but the added macro layer remains too weak to improve the approximately 50/100 source-groom appearance.
- r579 holds all geometry and lighting fixed and replaces the coverage Hair BSDF melanin response with explicit linear warm-brown values. The selected dark bound `(0.035, 0.012, 0.005)` raises the front head crop from 101.721 to 104.553 and the crown from 90.849 to 95.251 while face luminance remains 141.331. Mid and light bounds reach 106.930/110.093 head luminance and look increasingly orange, so both are rejected.
- r580 combines the selected direct response with 32 tangent cards. Its appearance is better balanced, but the first explicit audit finds 15,818 coverage points and 405 card vertices inside the body. r580 is rejected; a visual improvement cannot bypass collision.
- r581 projects all coverage points to 2.0 mm signed body clearance and card edge vertices to 2.5 mm. Frame 1 becomes clear and the outer cards pass all four frames, but 133 coverage points penetrate by up to 16.109 mm after body deformation at frames 20/33/61. Static-only projection is rejected.
- r582 iterates one shared rest shape against frames 1/20/33/61 twice. The saved 32-card bound preserves the five-view appearance and passes body, bandeau, binding and shorts checks with zero penetrating coverage points or card vertices at every frame. It is a collision basis, not a finished groom: 43,851 initial points and a large repeated multiframe set require projection, which exposes how unsuitable the raw r572 interpolation is as a direct runtime result.
- r583 sweeps the collision-safe direct colour by +10/+20/+30 percent. The selected +10 bound `(0.0385, 0.0132, 0.0055)` keeps face luminance at 141.333, raises the crown from 94.738 to 95.516 and avoids the orange cast of the stronger bounds.
- r584 writes the selected tone into a traceable 5,940-curve, 142,560-point coverage layer plus 32-card/1,536-vertex outer layer. The four-frame body/clothing audit passes with zero penetration and the 11-shot set passes without bright strips or new silhouette breaks. Strict hair score remains 50/100 and overall remains 58/100 because the outer hierarchy is subtle, the lob is still a straight curtain, the part is broad and regular, and there is no secondary motion. r584 is retained only as an offline tone/collision technical candidate; r517 remains the runtime hair candidate and website v83 is unchanged.

### v249r585–r587 — rejected dense-end hierarchy and collision-safe outer-card motion

- r585 groups the 5,940-curve coverage layer into 71 coarse families and moves only the lower 42% by 10/20/30/40 mm. The first three bounds average away inside the dense layer; 40 mm appears only as jagged rear ends. All bounds are rejected and dense coverage deformation is closed as a macro-lock route.
- r586 keeps r584 geometry, lights and colour fixed while multiplying only the owned outer-card alpha. The 1.5x bound is the highest clean setting and is carried into r587; 2.0x begins to expose isolated bright fibres, while 2.5x/3.0x reveal card strips. This is a material bound, not a score increase.
- r587 adds engine-drivable positive and negative shape targets to the 32 outer cards. The first peak produced 37 body penetrations at up to 1.366 mm; the final build clamps each companion-card pair to a shared collision-safe motion scale instead of relaxing the collision threshold. The resulting five authored keyframes have zero body, bandeau, binding or shorts penetration. Peak P95 motion is 6.781 mm, maximum is 7.908 mm, roots remain within 0.000016 mm, paired-card mismatch is 0.000120 mm and frame 61 settles within 0.000024 mm.
- The motion is real but visually subtle because only the outer 32 cards move. Strict hair remains 50/100 and overall 58/100: the straight curtain, broad regular part, uniform ends, static coverage and missing browser-performance proof still fail the hero gate. r587 is retained only as an offline dynamic/collision technical basis. r517 remains the runtime candidate and website v83 is unchanged.

### v249r588–r590 — dense-coverage hair architecture limits

- r588 staggers dense tail radius, widens all 32 outer cards and raises their endpoints. Whole-card widening immediately exposes horizontal card edges at the temples and crown; stronger bounds look like pasted strips and are rejected.
- r589 preserves the original card width through 54% length and changes only the lower silhouette. This avoids the root strips, but the 5,940-curve coverage layer averages the wider lower cards and endpoint hierarchy away. No bound produces a readable macro-lock silhouette.
- r590 keeps roots fixed and projects 628–645 near-part curves across the scalp by 8–14 mm. All directions remain nearly indistinguishable inside the dense mass. Dense-coverage deformation, whole-card widening and local part-flow displacement are closed as ineffective routes; r587 remains the offline dynamic hair basis at 50/100.

### v249r591–r597 — optical-aware outer-canthus collision bound

- r591 and r592 combine the visually cleaner three-iteration canthus fairing with posterior and anterior translations of the complete eye stack. Both directions increase cornea/sclera overlap and are rejected.
- r593 and r594 uniformly shrink the eye stack from 98% through 88%. Residual cornea overlap remains even when the eye size begins to become visibly too small, so global optical scaling is rejected.
- r595 finds that one canthus Laplacian iteration moves at most 0.554 mm but still creates 1–3 overlap pairs per optical layer. r596 scales that single step to 25%; even a 0.138 mm maximum move creates bilateral cornea overlap. The existing r569 optical boundary cannot accept a global fairing field.
- r597 applies one iteration, identifies the exact body triangles that overlap cornea or sclera, and rolls back only 16 responsible vertices. It retains 896 of 912 moved vertices and passes every face-layer overlap gate at frames 1/20/33/61 with no topology or dependency change. Strict comparison still shows too little improvement and the triangular canthus remains visible, so scores stay face 60, eyes 70 and overall 58. r597 is preserved as technical research and does not replace r569.

### v249r598–r602 — MPFB CC0 alternative face foundation

- r598 re-evaluates the separate MPFB CC0 v259/v260 branch. Its eyelid and canthus silhouettes are cleaner than r569, but the face is generic and unrigged. The v260 imported eye stack initially has 888 evaluated body-overlap pairs, so its clean render is not accepted as collision proof.
- r599 directly transfers the r510 skin material. It restores some surface response but depends on missing `AevryndAtlasUV`/`AevryndLipDetailUV` and old vertex attributes, producing unreliable tone and lip behavior. Direct transfer is rejected.
- r600 rebuilds skin from Aevrynd-owned object-space tone and pore nodes plus the CC0 `lips` vertex group. The neutral bound is the only retained material range; brighter bounds overexpose. It remains a research material because tone breakup and lips are too uniform.
- r601 sweeps eye scale 90/80/70% against depth -2/0/+2 mm. The largest tested zero-overlap fit is 90% of v260 size at +2 mm world-Y inset; 80% and 70% are unnecessarily small.
- r602 writes that eye fit and the neutral r600 material into a traceable 19,158-vertex, 18,486-polygon, 23-shape-key MPFB CC0 blend. Both sclerae, corneas and irises have zero static body overlap. Strict foundation scores are face 48, eye-topology appearance 62, skin 46 and overall 49. It is a long-term static reconstruction basis only: rig, dynamic collision, brows, lashes, tear film, oral layers, hair and clothing remain missing. r569/r587 and website v83 remain unchanged.

### v249r603–r610 — MPFB rig retarget and calibrated-lighting contract

- Directly scaling r602 to the 1.7208 m r569 body would require a 1.10907 multiplier but would place the pelvis about 127 mm above the old rig and enlarge the already broader shoulder/hip proportions. The body therefore stays at its authored 1.5516 m height; the Aevrynd 36-bone rig is refitted to MPFB's CC0 joint-marker groups instead.
- MPFB stores 13,380 visible-body vertices, 4,778 helper vertices and 1,000 joint-cube vertices in one mesh. Whole-mesh bone heat fails. r603 strips the helper islands into a temporary indexed proxy, computes bone-heat weights on the visible body, writes those weights back by original vertex index, rigidly assigns the 5,778 hidden helper vertices to the nearest fitted bone and explicitly normalizes all 19,158 vertices.
- r603 preserves all 23 existing body shape keys and binds both sclera/cornea/iris stacks to the refitted head bone. Frames 1/20/33/61 have zero body overlap for all six eye objects, no non-finite coordinates and a 1.06870 T-pose volume ratio. Maximum body motion is 0.46620 m. The strict deformation gate still fails: 16 subdivided edges around the bilateral shoulder/axilla transition stretch to 2.60070x against a hard <2.5 limit.
- r604 adjacency smoothing does not reach the disconnected weight islands and leaves the 2.60070x maximum unchanged. r605 armature-before-subdivision order worsens it to 2.65976x/28 severe edges. r606 parametric shoulder weights reach 2.87612x/106, r607 preserve-volume reaches 2.71214x/44, r609 spatial Gaussian smoothing reaches 2.77587x/66 and r610 bound cage Corrective Smooth reaches 2.75733x/42. r608 runs after subdivision, reports a 19,158-to-214,050 vertex-count mismatch and has no effect. All seven are rejected.
- A fixed review-lighting contract now removes inherited scene lighting from each candidate and uses Standard/None, exposure 0.0, a dark-neutral world and explicit Aevrynd key/fill/rim area lights. The r603 neutral and T-pose review no longer hides the lower body in black falloff and does not reproduce r602's clipped forehead. This calibrated rig is review-only and does not change the website or skin material.
- r603 is retained as rig-transfer research because it is the best numerical result, but it does not replace r602 or the r569 character mainline. The next rig pass must generate a bilateral pose-space shoulder corrective from the evaluated T pose and reduce all 16 outlier edges below 2.5 without changing the collision, normalization, shape-key or lighting contracts.

### v249r611–r631 — evaluated-space shoulder correctives and expanded collision gates

- r611-r613 drive bilateral post-skin Laplacian smoothing from the upper-arm quaternion. Even an intentionally extreme negative bound changes the 2.600698 maximum edge ratio by less than 0.00010, so generic relaxation is closed as ineffective. r614 applies a shape key in MPFB basis coordinates and misses the retargeted evaluated shoulder. Selecting the cage by its frame-1 evaluated position in r615 reaches the actual axillary band and reduces the failure to 2.541408/10 severe edges.
- r616 uses 0.14 bilateral lateral compression keys driven independently by each upper-arm quaternion. The original T-pose gate passes at 2.497022 maximum stretch, 0.839049 minimum compression, 1.069211 volume and zero severe edges. All original 23 body shape keys remain, with two new Aevrynd-owned shoulder keys.
- The first expanded action separates bilateral raise, left raise, right raise, high raise, torso twist, neck turn and raise-plus-twist. It exposes failures hidden by the old repeated T-pose frames: the initial high raise reaches 3.081568/368 severe edges and the combined torso interaction reaches 2.711384/13.
- r620-r627 test narrow, broad, lateral-only and lateral-plus-vertical high-range keys. Narrow keys move the maximum to their mask edge; excessive one-axis compression lowers the minimum edge ratio to 0.30. r628 retains a broad 0.80 lateral/0.60 vertical high-range correction. The high-raise frame then passes at 2.49 maximum, 0.38 minimum, 1.06 volume and zero severe edges.
- r630 adds one explicit interaction key for right arm raise under negative spine twist. In the r631 validation action, neutral plus seven independent and combined poses pass: global maximum edge ratio 2.497022, global minimum 0.311981, posed volume ratio 0.998755-1.069303, zero non-finite points and zero severe edges. All six sclera/cornea/iris objects remain body-clear in every frame.
- A separate evaluated-triangle BVH audit excludes only pairs sharing a vertex and reports zero non-adjacent body self-collision pairs across all eight frames. Fixed-light front, three-quarter and back renders show no shoulder hole, hard fold or bilateral break. The axillary and scapular muscle slide is still simplified, and wrist/hand posture remains weak, so strict rig visual quality is 60/100 rather than hero quality.
- r630 is retained as the authored rig/corrective technical candidate and r631 as its validation-action artifact. They supersede r603 for rig research, but do not replace r569 as the current complete character candidate or website v83. The MPFB face remains generic, skin remains flat, and hair, clothing and their collision envelopes are absent.

### v249r997–r998 — eye-depth limit, occluded eyelid relief and fixed brightness evidence

- Inspection of the r995 evaluated body finds zero boundary edges: the eye opening is backed by continuous manifold socket geometry rather than two open eyelid loops. A body-only diagnostic render confirms that the thin lower white rim and weak upper margin come from the visible transition between the outer face and this socket surface.
- r997 moves both complete eye stacks inward by 0.5/1.0/1.5 mm. The 0.5 mm bound stays body-clear but produces no meaningful visual improvement. At 1.0 mm the corneas produce 174 body-triangle overlaps; at 1.5 mm the sclerae and corneas produce 452. Eye depth is closed as the direct correction route.
- r998 adds one continuous-body eyelid-relief key and moves 152 socket-region vertices by at most 0.811 mm. Strengths 0.5/1.0/1.5 all remain clear of sclera, cornea, iris and the r995 lash layers, but the strongest render changes only 0.01963% of pixels with mean absolute delta 0.000000429. The selected vertices are visually occluded, so r998 is rejected without promotion.
- The fixed crop measures skin luminance 0.665378809 at every r998 strength, exactly 0.0% drift. The target-native branch has not become darker. The website v83 embedded base colour remains 0.5454 versus 0.5775 on the committed mainline, while dark hair framing and orbital shadow add perceptual contrast. Future visual candidates retain the strict plus-or-minus 1% regional luminance gate.

### v249r999–r1001 — active mixed-space eyelid relief

- r999 corrects the r998 world-Y inequality but still selects vertices in the unshaped MPFB Basis. It moves 138 source vertices by up to 0.680 mm and remains collision-free, yet produces negligible visible change. The failure reveals that the Basis mesh is not a valid spatial proxy for the rendered face.
- The authoritative inspection finds 22 active MPFB relative shape keys at frame 1. Their mixed pre-modifier surface contains 838 outer orbital-region vertices per eye, compared with only 146 in the same Basis-space query. r1000 computes this active mix before selecting vertices and writes a new relative key against the Basis, preserving the existing morph stack.
- r1000 moves 1,258 vertices by at most 0.713 mm and sweeps 0.5/1.0/1.5 strength. The full 1.0 bound creates the clearest restrained upper-lid ridge; 1.5 looks puffy and is rejected. The selected bound raises the fixed orbital skin crop by 0.13291%, safely inside the plus-or-minus 1% tone gate.
- r1001 retains strength 1.0. Frames 1/20/33/61 have zero body/sclera/cornea/lash triangle overlaps and zero non-adjacent body self-collision pairs. Front-view lid volume improves enough to move the strict local face score from about 47 to 48, but three-quarter and profile views expose inherited canthus holes, sclera wedges, shallow margin thickness and hair occlusion. r1001 is retained only as a local eyelid technical candidate and cannot replace website v83.

### v249r1002–r1007 — canthus closure limits and target-native caruncle layer

- r1002 pulls 826 recessed medial/lateral canthus vertices forward by up to 2.085 mm while preserving the manifold body. The change barely reduces the visible void and creates 21/39/65 lower-lash overlap pairs at half/full/strong strength. Every bound is rejected.
- r1003 exposes a bone-parent coordinate error: it writes variant positions through parent-space `location` after head binding, so the caruncles miss the intended world-space canthi. r1004 fixes placement with explicit world matrices. Its small bound is occluded, while medium/large bounds affect only a few pixels and create 44/96 body overlaps. r1005 thin forward ellipsoids remain closed volumes and worsen body overlap to 56/104/112.
- r1006 replaces closed ellipsoids with a four-ring, 24-segment patch per eye. Every vertex is ray-projected to the visible sclera and offset toward the camera. The small 1.0 x 0.6 mm radius bound has zero projection misses and zero body, sclera, cornea or lash overlap; medium and wide bounds fail from projection misses or overlap.
- r1007 retains the small patch as two explicitly named, procedurally owned, head-bone-bound caruncle layers. Frames 1/20/33/61 remain overlap-free. The layer reads only as a tiny pink medial tissue point in oblique views and the closest patch-to-cornea vertex distance is about 0.041 mm. It adds no strict score and does not repair the outer canthus void, so it remains an optional offline layer and website v83 is unchanged.


### v249r1008–r1010 — lateral-canthus rejection and open-topology recovery route

- r1008 isolates only the lateral canthus and moves 482 active-mix vertices by up to 2.788 mm at strength 1.0. Sclera, cornea, iris and upper lashes remain clear at every bound, but nonzero bounds create 24–27 lower-lash overlap pairs. Four-view review shows only negligible aperture change, so every bound is rejected.
- r1009 moves the front upper/lower bands that actually affect the rendered edge. Even the conservative bound creates 16 body/upper-lash overlap pairs; authored and strong bounds create 63/81. The visible gain remains too small and the round doll-like opening persists. It is rejected without rebuilding lashes.
- These two controlled failures reconfirm the earlier r437–r446 root cause: the current MPFB face is a closed manifold socket-backed surface, not a head with continuous upper/lower aperture loops. Coordinate edits can alter local shading but cannot produce a blink-ready canthus.
- A fresh r446 audit passes with zero regressions, so its true open-eye topology remains the reference. r1010 inventories r446, r981 and r1001 and finds that r981/r1001 share the corrected 38-bone rig while r446 has a different 21-bone rest skeleton. Directly copying hair, parenting or weights is invalid. The next build must retarget only the open eyelid-loop topology onto the current head and rig, reconnect it without a detached seam, rebuild both lash layers, and then run four-frame eye/lash/body and self-collision plus fixed-tone review.
- No skin material, light, view transform, exposure or website asset changes occur in r1008–r1010. These rejected geometry studies do not contribute to progressive darkening; website v83 remains 5.56% darker in embedded base color than the committed mainline.


### v249r1011–r1015 — visible-margin retarget and true-aperture proof

- r1011 extracts the only naked r446 eye boundary: one closed 52-edge loop per side. Eye-frame normalization maps every point to a unique r1001 evaluated vertex at about 0.410 mm median and 0.746 mm worst distance, but the loop sits at mean world Y -0.0888 behind the eyes. It proves the socket is open but is not the visible lid edge. r1012 confirms the mapped loop is occluded and rejects it as a margin source.
- r1013 transfers the old authored 41-point upper/lower visible-margin curves. Their width is useful, but the upper curve lies only 0.06-0.18 eye radii above center and crosses the current iris; upper/lower endpoints also remain open. Raw transfer is rejected.
- r1014 rebuilds exact-closure Aevrynd-owned anatomical guides with the lateral canthus above the medial. The restrained 6.710 mm aperture is too narrow and the open 9.310 mm bound trends back toward doll-eye proportions. The balanced 7.834 mm guide is selected as the topology target.
- r1015 removes only 77 camera-facing body faces inside that guide: 39 left and 38 right. It preserves all 19,158 vertices and all 30 shape-key blocks byte-index compatible, while frame-1 body/sclera/cornea/iris/upper/lower-lash triangle overlap stays zero. This proves the current mesh can carry a true aperture without discarding the corrected rig or morph stack.
- Strict front and three-quarter review rejects r1015 visually: the unbridged upper boundary is faceted, white globe edges are exposed, and the canthi show dark holes. The next topology build must extrude a continuous inward lid wall, copy shape-key and vertex-group data to its new vertices, fit the inner rim outside the eye envelope, rebuild lashes and then run frames 1/20/33/61 plus body self-collision. No website promotion is allowed.
- r1011-r1015 change no skin material, lights, view transform, exposure or website asset, so they add no progressive darkening.

### v249r1016–r1019 — contiguous aperture and inner-wall limits

- r1016 extrudes 0.35/0.65/1.00 mm inner-wall bounds from r1015. The source cut exposes 118 left and 116 right boundary edges across many disconnected islands, so every bound renders stepped dark seams. All are rejected even though the static overlap gate passes.
- r1017 removes the camera-facing normal filter, builds shared-edge face components inside the balanced guide and deletes only the largest component per eye. It preserves all 19,158 vertices and 30 shape keys, removes 30 left plus 29 right faces, and produces exactly one 24-edge cut boundary on each side. Frames 1/20/33/61 keep sclera, cornea, iris and both lash layers body-clear, with zero upper/lower-lash mutual overlap and no non-finite lash vertices.
- r1017 is a topology proof rather than a visual candidate. The connected opening removes the fragmented seam, but its 24-edge contour is visibly coarse and still exposes the white globe edge because no supporting lid rings exist.
- r1018 extrudes only the identified 24-edge visible rings. The 0.35 mm wall adds 48 weighted vertices and remains overlap-free, but the visual gain is negligible. The 0.65/1.00 mm bounds create 26/44 body-to-eye-layer overlaps and are rejected.
- r1019 moves the 48 visible ring vertices toward the owned r1014 balanced anatomical guide while applying identical corrections to all shape-key blocks. Restrained, balanced and authored strengths move at most 0.744/1.382/2.126 mm and all remain frame-1 overlap-free. None removes the coarse rim or raises strict face quality, so all are rejected.
- The next valid construction requires a denser local eyelid patch with separate upper/lower margin loops and at least two supporting rings. It must transfer the existing 30 shape-key deltas and deform weights, reconnect without a visible seam, rebuild lashes, then pass frames 1/20/33/61 and self-collision. Website v83 remains unchanged.
- r1016-r1019 do not alter skin material, lighting, view transform or exposure. The fixed-tone evidence still shows no progressive mainline darkening.

### v249r1020–r1022 — dense attached eyelid patch and wetline optics

- r1020 subdivides each r1017 24-edge opening into a 48-edge visible margin, projects it 35% toward the owned balanced guide, and extrudes two attached supporting rings. It adds 48 subdivided vertices plus 192 weighted ring vertices while keeping all 30 shape-key blocks at the final 19,398-vertex length.
- The r1020a 0.14/0.25 mm support depths pass frames 1/20/33/61 with zero body overlap against sclera, cornea, iris and both lash layers. r1020b and r1020c intersect both corneas at every frame, totaling 176 and 256 triangle-overlap pairs, so the usable depth envelope is closed at r1020a.
- r1020a is retained as the new local eyelid-topology base. Its 48-edge margin is smoother than r1017, but strict integrated face quality stays 48/100 because the canthi remain open, upper-lid volume is weak and the source face remains generic.
- r1021 moves only the innermost ring by 0.20/0.35/0.50 mm near both canthi and gives its 96 polygons a coated rose material. Every bound remains four-frame collision-free, but the margin becomes a bright pink plastic strip and the dark canthus void remains. All are visually rejected.
- r1022 freezes the r1020a geometry and removes coat, lowers IOR level, and sweeps three much darker rose responses. The dry-rose bound is retained as a local material candidate because it removes the plastic highlight; it does not raise the overall face score. Integrated brows-and-lashes review remains synthetic with faint lashes and unresolved canthi.
- Fixed-review tone remains stable from r1020a to r1022a: forehead -0.000005%, left cheek -0.000496% and right cheek -0.000390%, all far inside the plus-or-minus 1% gate. These iterations do not progressively darken the character, and website v83 remains unchanged.
- The next eye pass must build explicit medial/lateral closure wedges outside the cornea envelope, add upper-lid levator volume and orbital-fat transition, then refit denser lashes. Margin material tuning alone is closed.
